** fanshi/org-gtd-ai-delegate-project-scope
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! org-gtd
  (defun fanshi/org-gtd-ai-delegate-project-scope (&optional prompt)
    (when (fanshi/delegated-to-ai-p)
      (let ((project (completing-read (or prompt "Select project: ")
                                      (projectile-known-projects)
                                      (lambda (project) (not (string-match "/\\.trees" project))))))
        (if (projectile-project-p project)
            (progn (message "Scoped to project: %s" project) project)
          (let* ((task-heading (org-get-heading t t t t))
                 (project-name (replace-regexp-in-string "^-+\\|-+$" ""
                                                         (format "ai-%s" (replace-regexp-in-string
                                                                          "[^a-z0-9-]+" "-"
                                                                          (downcase (string-trim task-heading))))))
                 (project-dir (expand-file-name project-name fanshi/breeding-ground-dir))
                 (default-directory project-dir))
            (make-directory project-dir t)
            (call-process "jj" nil nil nil "git" "init")
            (projectile-add-known-project project-dir)
            (org-entry-put (point) "SCOPED_PROJECT" project-dir)
            (message "Created project: %s" project-dir)
            project-dir))))))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-task-state-machine
Define the TODO keyword sequence and properties for AI-delegated tasks.
This is the single source of truth for task state management.
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(after! org
  ;; Add custom TODO keywords for AI delegation workflow
  ;; State machine: TODO → DELEGATED → DONE / KILL
  (add-to-list 'org-todo-keywords
               '(sequence "TODO" "DELEGATED" "|" "DONE" "KILL"))

  (defconst fanshi/ai-task-properties
    '("WORKTREE_DIR" "MAIN_WORKSPACE" "INTEGRATED" "AGENT_BUF" "DELEGATED_TO"
      "DELEGATED_AT" "SCOPED_PROJECT"
      "PRIORITY" "TIME_LIMIT" "DEADLINE" "SCHEDULE" "AGENT" "DEPENDS_ON")
    "List of properties used to track AI-delegated tasks in org entries.
WORKTREE_DIR: Path to the jj workspace for this task.
MAIN_WORKSPACE: Where the work lands, recorded at delegation time so that
                integration and discard need not guess it from WORKTREE_DIR.
INTEGRATED:   Timestamp set once the agent's commit has been squashed into
              the main workspace; makes `fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task'
              refuse a second squash.
AGENT_BUF: Name of the gptel-agent buffer handling this task.
DELEGATED_TO: The gptel preset name (e.g., coding, product, scrum).
              Set to 'user' when the task is pending human input.
DELEGATED_AT: Timestamp when the task was delegated.
SCOPED_PROJECT: The project directory this task belongs to.
PRIORITY: \"A\"-\"E\", A most urgent.
TIME_LIMIT: Seconds the agent may spend on the task.
DEADLINE: \"YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]\" by when the task must be done.
SCHEDULE: \"YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]\" when the task should start.
AGENT: The gptel preset name to delegate this task to (e.g., coding, research).
DEPENDS_ON: Comma-separated org-ids of tasks that must be DONE before this one.")

  (defun fanshi/scrum-todo-change-handler ()
    "Handler for org-todo-keyword-change-hook.
Logs messages and triggers actions based on AI task state transitions.
Only fires for entries that have a WORKTREE_DIR property."
    (when (org-entry-get (point) "WORKTREE_DIR")
      (let* ((task-id (org-entry-get (point) "ID"))
             (worktree (org-entry-get (point) "WORKTREE_DIR"))
             (heading (org-get-heading t t t t)))
        (pcase org-state
          ("DELEGATED"
           (message "AI Task delegated: '%s' → worktree %s" heading worktree))
          ("DONE"
           (message "AI Task completed: '%s'" heading)
           (when task-id
             (message "Task ID: %s" task-id)))
          ("KILL"
           (message "AI Task killed: '%s'" heading)
           ;; (when (and worktree (file-directory-p worktree))
           ;;   (when (y-or-n-p (format "Clean up worktree %s? " worktree))
           ;;     (message "Cleaning up worktree...")
           ;;     (call-process "git" nil nil nil "worktree" "prune")))
           )
          (_ nil)))))
  ;; FIXME local hook?
  (add-hook 'org-after-todo-state-change-hook #'fanshi/scrum-todo-change-handler))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-delegate--update-task-by-id
Org-ID based task update: the primary mechanism for task state transitions.

The jj lifecycle around it, since the commands in this section are the ones
that touch the user's working copy:
- ~Delegate~ creates the agent workspace under ~<repo>/.trees/<branch>~ and
  records ~WORKTREE_DIR~ plus ~MAIN_WORKSPACE~ on the entry, so nothing
  downstream has to guess where the work lands.
- The agent works in its own workspace; ~FinishTask~ only describes its ~@~,
  ~AbortTask~ only marks it ~ABORTED: ...~.  Neither destroys anything.
- ~fanshi/ai-delegate--post-response-integrate~ calls
  ~fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task~, which ~squash~es the agent's ~@~
  into ~@~ of the main workspace (a ~rebase~ would leave the changes as a
  sibling commit the user still has to pull in by hand), marks the entry
  ~INTEGRATED~, forgets the workspace and trashes its directory.
- Review (~--review-task-diff~, ~--review-task-log~) and ~--discard-task~
  stay interactive human commands.
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(defvar-local fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id nil
  "The org-id of the current AI-delegated task.")

(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--update-task-by-id (task-id status summary)
    "Update the task identified by TASK-ID with STATUS and SUMMARY.
STATUS is a TODO keyword like \"DONE\" or \"KILL\".
Appends the summary as a timestamped note and sets the TODO state.
Works even if the org file is not currently visited in a buffer."
    (unless task-id
      (error "No task-id provided"))
    (org-id-goto task-id)
    (org-todo status)
    (org-end-of-subtree t t)
    (insert (format "\n- %s [%s]: %s"
                    (format-time-string "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
                    status
                    summary))
    (save-buffer)
    (message "Task %s: %s — %s" task-id status summary))

  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property (task-id property &optional value)
    "Read the org PROPERTY of TASK-ID without switching buffers or windows.
With VALUE, set the property instead and return VALUE.  Org entries live in
files that may not be visited, so this goes through the org-id marker."
    (when-let* ((marker (org-id-find task-id t))
                (buf (marker-buffer marker)))
      (with-current-buffer buf
        (save-excursion
          (goto-char marker)
          (prog1
              (if value
                  (progn
                    (org-entry-put (point) property value)
                    (save-buffer)
                    value)
                (org-entry-get (point) property))
            (move-marker marker nil))))))

  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--retarget-agent-buffer (task-id main-dir)
    "Point TASK-ID's agent buffer at MAIN-DIR once its workspace is gone.
The buffer's `default-directory' is the workspace just trashed, so any later
shell or file command in it would die with \"Filename does not exist\".
MAIN-DIR is where the work landed; the message makes it visible that a
conversation continued in that buffer is no longer isolated."
    (when-let* ((name (fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property task-id "AGENT_BUF"))
                (buf (get-buffer name)))
      (with-current-buffer buf
        (setq default-directory main-dir)
        (message "Agent buffer %s now points at %s (its workspace is gone)"
                 (buffer-name buf) (abbreviate-file-name main-dir)))))

  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--integration-message (main-dir agent-desc task-id)
    "Build the squash description for integration in MAIN-DIR.
`jj squash --message' REPLACES the destination's description, so the
user's own description on @ has to be carried over by hand: jj would
otherwise open its editor to combine two non-empty descriptions, and an
editor raised from a post-response hook hangs Emacs.  AGENT-DESC is the
agent's description (its FinishTask summary); when both sides are empty a
generated one-liner is used.  MAIN-DIR is where the destination @ lives.
Called from the agent buffer, so MAIN-DIR is passed explicitly to
`fanshi/call-jj' rather than left to the current `default-directory'."
    (let ((dest (string-trim
                 (fanshi/call-jj main-dir "log" "-r" "@" "--no-graph"
                                 "-T" "description")))
          (agent (string-trim (or agent-desc ""))))
      (cond
       ((and (string-empty-p dest) (string-empty-p agent))
        (format "integrate task %s" task-id))
       ((string-empty-p dest) agent)
       ((string-empty-p agent) dest)
       (t (format "%s\n\n%s" dest agent))))))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-delegate
integration happens automatically via `fanshi/ai-delegate--post-response-integrate' (which squashes the agent's @ into @ of the main workspace, marks the entry INTEGRATED and cleans the workspace up), but `fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task' stays interactive for the case where the auto-integration failed and the workspace still exists
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--main-workspace-dir (task-id worktree-dir)
    "Return the main workspace directory for TASK-ID's agent WORKTREE-DIR.
The MAIN_WORKSPACE property recorded at delegation time wins.  Otherwise fall
back to the layout `fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-delegate' creates,
<repo>/.trees/<branch>: two levels up.  `expand-file-name' so a relative
property still yields a usable `default-directory'."
    (let ((recorded (fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property task-id "MAIN_WORKSPACE")))
      (file-name-as-directory
       (expand-file-name
        (if (and recorded (file-directory-p recorded))
            recorded
          (directory-file-name
           (file-name-directory (file-name-directory worktree-dir))))))))

  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task (task-id)
    "Integrate the agent's changes from TASK-ID into the main workspace.
Moves the diff of the agent's working-copy commit into @ of the main
workspace with `jj squash --from <agent> --into @', so the changes actually
land in the user's working copy instead of merely being rebased as a sibling
child of it.  The description is passed explicitly (see
`fanshi/ai-delegate--integration-message'); `--message=' over `-m' so a
leading hyphen in the text is not read as a flag.  The entry is marked
INTEGRATED before the workspace is forgotten and its directory trashed, which
makes a retry refuse instead of squashing an empty commit into @.
jj records a conflict instead of failing, so the result is checked and
reported: the main workspace then contains conflict markers."
    (interactive "sTask ID: ")
    (let ((worktree-dir (fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property task-id "WORKTREE_DIR")))
      (unless (and worktree-dir (file-directory-p worktree-dir))
        (error "No workspace found for task %s" task-id))
      (when-let ((done (fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property task-id "INTEGRATED")))
        (error "Task %s was already integrated on %s" task-id done))
      (let* ((main-dir (fanshi/ai-delegate--main-workspace-dir task-id worktree-dir))
             ;; The agent's working-copy id and its full description, split on
             ;; the FIRST tab only: a description may contain tabs, and those
             ;; belong to the message, not to the separator.
             (agent-line (string-trim
                          (fanshi/call-jj worktree-dir
                                          "log" "-r" "@" "--no-graph" "-T"
                                          "commit_id() ++ \"\\t\" ++ description ++ \"\\n\""))))
        (sep (string-match "\t" agent-line))
        (commit-id (if sep (substring agent-line 0 sep) ""))
        (agent-desc (if sep (substring agent-line (1+ sep)) "")))
      (unless (> (length commit-id) 0)
        (error "No working-copy commit found in workspace %s" worktree-dir))
      ;; All jj calls below pass MAIN-DIR to `fanshi/call-jj': this function is
      ;; reachable from a post-response hook in the agent buffer, whose cwd is
      ;; the agent's workspace, not MAIN-DIR.
      (fanshi/call-jj main-dir "squash" "--from" commit-id "--into" "@"
                      (format "--message=%s"
                              (fanshi/ai-delegate--integration-message
                               main-dir agent-desc task-id)))
      ;; Marked before the cleanup below: a half-finished integration must
      ;; never be "helpfully" re-run into a second squash.
      (fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property task-id "INTEGRATED"
                                         (format-time-string "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"))
      ;; jj records a conflict instead of failing, so detect it and tell the
      ;; user: the main workspace now contains conflict markers.
      (let ((conflicted
             (string= "true"
                      (string-trim
                       (fanshi/call-jj main-dir "log" "-r" "@" "--no-graph" "-T"
                                       "conflict()")))))
        ;; Cleanup failures must not mask a successful integration, so each
        ;; step is best-effort on its own: `workspace forget' only drops
        ;; jj's bookkeeping, trashing removes the files, and neither may
        ;; skip the other.
        (condition-case err
            (fanshi/call-jj main-dir "workspace" "forget"
                            (file-name-nondirectory
                             (directory-file-name worktree-dir)))
          (error
           (message "Integrated task %s, but `jj workspace forget' failed: %s"
                    task-id (error-message-string err))))
        (condition-case err
            (system-move-file-to-trash worktree-dir)
          (error
           (message "Integrated task %s, but trashing %s failed: %s"
                    task-id worktree-dir (error-message-string err))))
        (fanshi/ai-delegate--retarget-agent-buffer task-id main-dir)
        (if conflicted
            (progn
              (message "Integrated task %s WITH CONFLICTS: resolve them in %s (jj resolve)"
                       task-id main-dir)
              (format "Integrated task %s into %s but the result is CONFLICTED: run 'jj status' and 'jj resolve' in %s."
                      task-id main-dir main-dir))
          (message "Integrated changes from task %s" task-id)
          (format "Successfully integrated changes from task %s into %s. Run 'jj git push' when ready."
                  task-id main-dir)))))

  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--post-response-integrate (beg end)
    "Auto-integrate the agent's commit once its task becomes DONE.
Runs in the delegated sub-agent buffer after each response (BEG and END
mark the response and are unused).  Aborted (KILL) tasks are left for
the human to discard."
    (when-let* ((task-id fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id)
                (marker (org-id-find task-id t))
                (buf (marker-buffer marker))
                (state (with-current-buffer buf
                         (save-excursion
                           (goto-char marker)
                           (org-get-todo-state)))))
      (move-marker marker nil)
      (when (string= state "DONE")
        ;; Disarm before mutating: integration runs jj in the user's main
        ;; workspace, so a failure must never be retried on every later
        ;; response of this buffer.  The message points at the manual path.
        (remove-hook 'gptel-post-response-functions
                     #'fanshi/ai-delegate--post-response-integrate t)
        (setq-local fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id nil)
        (condition-case err
            (progn
              (fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task task-id)
              (message "Auto-integrated changes for task %s" task-id))
          (error
           (message "Failed to auto-integrate task %s: %s (inspect with 'jj log' in %s, then M-x fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task)"
                    task-id (error-message-string err)
                    (abbreviate-file-name default-directory)))))))

  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-delegate (cb task agent &optional task-id priority time-limit deadline schedule)
    "Write TASK into the AI tasks backlog and delegate it to AGENT.
CB is the async result callback (see `gptel--handle-tool-use').
If TASK-ID is given, the task already exists in the org file and is
delegated in place; otherwise a new TODO entry is created first.
PRIORITY (\"A\"-\"E\"), TIME-LIMIT (seconds), DEADLINE and SCHEDULE
(\"YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]\") are stored as org properties when given.
If AGENT is \"user\", the task is marked as pending human input, using
TASK as the description of what is needed.
Otherwise, spawn a dedicated gptel-agent buffer with AGENT
working on TASK in an isolated jj workspace."
    (condition-case err
        (if (string= (if (symbolp agent) (symbol-name agent) agent) "user")
            ;; Delegate to human
            (if task-id
                (progn
                  (org-id-goto task-id)
                  (org-entry-put (point) "DELEGATED_TO" "user")
                  (org-end-of-subtree t t)
                  (insert (format "\n- %s [USER]: %s"
                                  (format-time-string "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
                                  task))
                  (save-buffer)
                  (message "Task delegated to user: %s" task)
                  (funcall cb (format "Task delegated to user: %s" task)))
              (error "Cannot delegate to user without task-id"))
          ;; Delegate to AI agent
          (unless task-id
            (setq task-id
                  (fanshi/ai-task--capture
                   (s-truncate 60 (replace-regexp-in-string "[\n\r]" " " (string-trim task)))
                   task
                   (list (when priority (cons "PRIORITY" priority))
                         (cons "TIME_LIMIT" (number-to-string (or time-limit fanshi/ai-task-time-limit)))
                         (when deadline (cons "DEADLINE" deadline))
                         (when schedule (cons "SCHEDULE" schedule))))))
          (let* ((task-marker (org-id-find task-id 'marker))
                 (depends-on (and task-marker
                                  (org-entry-get task-marker "DEPENDS_ON")))
                 ;; Expand relative paths against the delegating buffer's
                 ;; default-directory up front so `file-directory-p' checks the
                 ;; right location.
                 (scoped-project (and task-marker
                                      (let ((p (org-entry-get task-marker "SCOPED_PROJECT")))
                                        (and p (expand-file-name p)))))
                 (project-root (or (and scoped-project (file-directory-p scoped-project)
                                        scoped-project)
                                   (projectile-project-root)))
                 (branch-name (format "task-%s-%s" task-id (format-time-string "%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")))
                 (target-dir (expand-file-name (concat ".trees/" branch-name) project-root)))
            ;; A task-id the model made up (or that was archived) would
            ;; otherwise silently skip the gate below, so treat it as fatal.
            (unless task-marker
              (error "Task %s not found" task-id))
            ;; Hard dependency gate: the nightly prompt telling the Scrum
            ;; Master to skip blocked tasks is a soft guarantee; this is not.
            ;; DEPENDS_ON is stored comma-separated, so split on both.  Most
            ;; tasks carry no dependency at all, hence the `or'.
            (dolist (dep (split-string (or depends-on "") "[, ]+"))
              (unless (string-blank-p dep)
                (let ((dep-m (org-id-find dep 'marker)))
                  (cond
                   ((not dep-m)
                    (error "Task %s has a dependency %s that does not exist" task-id dep))
                   ((not (string= (org-entry-get dep-m "TODO") "DONE"))
                    (error "Task %s is blocked by %s which is not DONE" task-id dep))))))
            ;; 1. Create jj workspace if needed.
            ;; jj derives the repo and the workspace context from its working
            ;; directory, so every command passes it explicitly: the
            ;; repo-level one runs in PROJECT-ROOT (also the main workspace
            ;; here), the rest inside the new workspace.  The directory is
            ;; passed explicitly to `fanshi/call-jj' (a NIL directory would
            ;; fall back to the invoking buffer's `default-directory', which is
            ;; not necessarily the repo or the new workspace).  A non-zero exit
            ;; becomes an error carrying jj's own message, so the model sees the
            ;; reason instead of a bare exit code.
            (unless (file-directory-p target-dir)
              (make-directory (file-name-directory target-dir) t)
              ;; `workspace add' checks the new workspace out on top of the
              ;; PARENTS of this workspace's @: the agent starts from the
              ;; same base commit as the main workspace, deliberately without
              ;; the user's uncommitted changes in @ (which is exactly what
              ;; lets `fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task' squash the work
              ;; back in cleanly).  `--message=' records the task as that
              ;; commit's description.  (The old `jj new main@' assumed a
              ;; workspace literally named "main"; jj names the first
              ;; workspace after the repo, so that revspec usually failed and
              ;; the description was lost.  `--message=' over `-m' because
              ;; clap would read a leading hyphen in the task text as a
              ;; flag; `-m' also requires a jj new enough to support it.)
              (fanshi/call-jj project-root "workspace" "add"
                              (format "--message=%s" task) target-dir)
              ;; Identity is per-workspace (`--workspace'): all agent
              ;; workspaces share one repo, so `--repo' would let the last
              ;; delegation overwrite every earlier agent's name.
              (fanshi/call-jj target-dir "config" "set" "--workspace" "user.name"
                              (format "Agent (%s)" agent))
              (fanshi/call-jj target-dir "config" "set" "--workspace" "user.email"
                              (format "%s-agent@local" agent)))
            ;; Hard preset gate: validate BEFORE creating the jj workspace so a
            ;; bad agent name cannot leave an orphan workspace behind.  The
            ;; lookup goes through `gptel-get-preset' directly because
            ;; gptel-agent's own registry cannot resolve the preset here
            ;; (`gptel-agent-dirs' is nil and `gptel-agent-update' is ignored
            ;; in this config); mirrors the check in
            ;; `fanshi/gptel-agent--task'.
            (unless (gptel-get-preset (intern-soft (if (symbolp agent) (symbol-name agent) agent)))
              (error "gptel preset %s not found" agent))
            ;; 2. Create the child agent buffer.
            ;; `gptel-agent' creates its OWN gptel buffer and returns it;
            ;; we must configure that buffer (not a separately-named one).
            ;; `gptel-agent's AGENT-PRESET argument names a gptel preset, so
            ;; pass AGENT as a symbol (not a list); it resolves it via
            ;; `gptel-get-preset', bypassing gptel-agent's own (empty) agent
            ;; registry.
            (let ((agent-buf (gptel-agent target-dir
                                          (intern (if (symbolp agent) (symbol-name agent) agent)))))
              ;; 3. Update org entry with DELEGATED status and properties
              (save-excursion
                (org-id-goto task-id)
                (when priority (org-entry-put (point) "PRIORITY" priority))
                (when time-limit (org-entry-put (point) "TIME_LIMIT" (number-to-string time-limit)))
                (when deadline (org-entry-put (point) "DEADLINE" deadline))
                (when schedule (org-entry-put (point) "SCHEDULE" schedule))
                (org-entry-put (point) "WORKTREE_DIR" target-dir)
                ;; Where the work lands: integration and discard must not have
                ;; to guess the main workspace from the shape of WORKTREE_DIR.
                (when project-root
                  (org-entry-put (point) "MAIN_WORKSPACE"
                                 (file-name-as-directory project-root)))
                (org-entry-put (point) "DELEGATED_TO" (if (symbolp agent) (symbol-name agent) agent))
                (org-entry-put (point) "DELEGATED_AT" (format-time-string "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"))
                (org-entry-put (point) "AGENT_BUF" (buffer-name agent-buf))
                (org-todo "DELEGATED")
                (save-buffer))
              ;; 4. Configure the agent buffer and send the task
              (with-current-buffer agent-buf
                ;; NOTE: `fanshi/gptel-jj-workspace' is deliberately left nil
                ;; so the post-response auto-commit hook
                ;; (`fanshi/gptel--jj-auto-commit') stays off in this buffer:
                ;; it would commit each response and leave an empty @ behind,
                ;; and `fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task' integrates @ only,
                ;; so the work would silently be dropped at integration time.
                (setq-local fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id task-id)
                ;; Auto-integrate the agent's commit once the task becomes DONE
                (add-hook 'gptel-post-response-functions
                          #'fanshi/ai-delegate--post-response-integrate nil t)
                (goto-char (point-max))
                (insert
                 (string-join
                  (list "# Delegated Task"
                        ""
                        task
                        ""
                        (format "You are working in the isolated jj workspace %s (workspace name \"%s\"), based on the commit the main workspace was on."
                                (file-name-as-directory target-dir) branch-name)
                        "jj snapshots your file changes into the working-copy commit (@) automatically; FinishTask hands that commit over for integration, so do NOT run `jj new`, `jj squash` or `jj abandon` yourself."
                        "When you complete the task, call the FinishTask tool with a summary."
                        "If you cannot complete it, call AbortTask with a reason: your workspace and its partial work stay on disk for the human to review or discard."
                        "If you need human input, call Delegate with agent=\"user\" and a task description."
                        "")
                  "\n"))
                (gptel-send))
              (funcall cb (format "Task delegated to agent buffer '%s' in jj workspace.\nOrg entry updated to DELEGATED.\nYou can observe progress by visiting the agent buffer."
                                  (buffer-name agent-buf))))))
      (error
       (funcall cb
                (format "Error delegating task: %s"
                        (if (stringp err)
                            err
                          (error-message-string err)))))))
  (gptel-make-tool
   :name "Delegate"
   :function #'fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-delegate
   :description "Write a task into the AI tasks backlog and delegate it, in one step.
TASK: description of the task (also what the human needs to provide when agent is \"user\").
AGENT: name of the agent preset to delegate to (e.g. \"coding\", \"research\"), or \"user\" for human input.
TASK-ID: org-id of an existing backlog entry; omit it to create a new entry first.
PRIORITY: \"A\"-\"E\", A most urgent.
TIME-LIMIT: seconds the agent may spend on the task.
DEADLINE: \"YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]\" by when the task must be done.
SCHEDULE: \"YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]\" when the task should start."
   :args '((:name "task" :type string
            :description "Description of the task to delegate, or what human input is needed")
           (:name "agent" :type string
            :description "Name of the agent preset to delegate to, or \"user\" for human input")
           (:name "task-id" :type string :optional t
            :description "The org-id of an existing backlog entry; omit to create a new task entry")
           (:name "priority" :type string :optional t :enum ["A" "B" "C" "D" "E"]
            :description "Priority, A most urgent to E least urgent")
           (:name "time-limit" :type integer :optional t
            :description "Seconds the agent may spend on the task")
           (:name "deadline" :type string :optional t
            :description "When the task must be done, YYYY-MM-DD or YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM")
           (:name "schedule" :type string :optional t
            :description "When the task should start, YYYY-MM-DD or YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM"))
   :category "agents"
   :async t))
#+end_src
** Review/integration/discard are human decisions:
*** fanshi/ai-delegate--discard-task
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--discard-task (task-id)
    "Discard all changes from TASK-ID's agent workspace.
Abandons the working-copy commit, forgets the workspace and removes the
directory.  Each step is best-effort on its own: the point of discard is to
get the workspace gone, so an already-abandoned @ (AbortTask leaves it
alone now) or a stale workspace must not stop the cleanup."
    (interactive "sTask ID: ")
    (let ((worktree-dir (fanshi/ai-delegate--task-property task-id "WORKTREE_DIR")))
      (unless (and worktree-dir (file-directory-p worktree-dir))
        (error "No workspace found for task %s" task-id))
      (let ((main-dir (fanshi/ai-delegate--main-workspace-dir task-id worktree-dir))
            (workspace-name (file-name-nondirectory (directory-file-name worktree-dir))))
        ;; Abandon @ in the agent's workspace; `fanshi/call-jj' keeps jj's
        ;; output out of the current buffer, reports why it failed, and pins the
        ;; workspace it acts on.
        (condition-case err
            (fanshi/call-jj worktree-dir "abandon" "@")
          (error
           (message "Discarding task %s: `jj abandon' failed (%s), cleaning up anyway"
                    task-id (error-message-string err))))
        ;; Forget the workspace before trashing the directory it lives in.
        (condition-case err
            (fanshi/call-jj main-dir "workspace" "forget" workspace-name)
          (error
           (message "Discarded task %s, but `jj workspace forget' failed: %s"
                    task-id (error-message-string err))))
        (condition-case err
            (system-move-file-to-trash worktree-dir)
          (error
           (message "Discarded task %s, but trashing %s failed: %s"
                    task-id worktree-dir (error-message-string err))))
        (fanshi/ai-delegate--retarget-agent-buffer task-id main-dir)
        (message "Discarded changes from task %s" task-id)
        (format "Discarded changes and removed workspace for task %s" task-id)))))
#+end_src
*** fanshi/ai-delegate--review-task-diff
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(defun fanshi/ai-delegate--review-task-diff (task-id)
    "Review the changes made by an agent for TASK-ID.
Opens a buffer showing the diff of the agent's working-copy commit."
    (interactive "sTask ID: ")
    (save-excursion
      (org-id-goto task-id)
      (let ((worktree-dir (org-entry-get (point) "WORKTREE_DIR")))
        (unless (and worktree-dir (file-directory-p worktree-dir))
          (error "No workspace found for task %s" task-id))
        (let ((diff-buf (get-buffer-create (format "*jj diff: %s*" task-id))))
          ;; The diff is captured from WORKTREE-DIR by passing it to
          ;; `fanshi/call-jj': the review can be invoked from anywhere (a gptel
          ;; tool call, an Org headline in the tasks file), so the workspace is
          ;; named rather than taken from the current directory.
          (let ((diff (fanshi/call-jj worktree-dir "diff")))
            (with-current-buffer diff-buf
              ;; Erase first: the buffer is reused, so a second review must not
              ;; append to the previous diff.
              (let ((inhibit-read-only t))
                (erase-buffer)
                (insert diff)
                (diff-mode))))
          (switch-to-buffer-other-window diff-buf)
          (message "Showing jj diff for task %s" task-id)
          (format "Diff shown in buffer for task %s" task-id)))))
#+end_src
*** fanshi/ai-delegate--review-task-log
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--review-task-log (task-id)
    "Review the commit log for TASK-ID's workspace.
Shows the commit graph including the agent's changes."
    (interactive "sTask ID: ")
    (save-excursion
      (org-id-goto task-id)
      (let ((worktree-dir (org-entry-get (point) "WORKTREE_DIR")))
        (unless (and worktree-dir (file-directory-p worktree-dir))
          (error "No workspace found for task %s" task-id))
        (let ((log-buf (get-buffer-create (format "*jj log: %s*" task-id))))
          ;; Captured from WORKTREE-DIR by passing it to `fanshi/call-jj': the
          ;; review can be invoked from anywhere (a gptel tool call, an Org
          ;; headline in the tasks file), so the workspace is named rather than
          ;; taken from the current directory.
          (let ((log (fanshi/call-jj worktree-dir
                                     "log" "-T"
                                     "commit_id.short() ++ \" \" ++ description.firstline() ++ \"\\n\"")))
            (with-current-buffer log-buf
              (let ((inhibit-read-only t))
                (erase-buffer)
                (insert log)
                (special-mode))))
          (switch-to-buffer-other-window log-buf)
          (message "Showing jj log for task %s" task-id)
          (format "Log shown in buffer for task %s" task-id))))))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-finish-task
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-finish-task (summary)
    "Tool for the gptel-agent to signal task completion.
SUMMARY is a brief description of what was accomplished."
    (when fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id
      ;; Record SUMMARY as the description of @ (in this buffer's workspace);
      ;; `fanshi/ai-delegate--integrate-task' reads it back as the squash
      ;; message.  A failure is only a warning: the task is still DONE and
      ;; integration falls back to a generated description.
      (condition-case err
          (fanshi/call-jj nil "describe" (format "--message=%s" summary))
        (error
         (message "Warning: jj describe failed: %s" (error-message-string err))))
      (fanshi/ai-delegate--update-task-by-id fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id "DONE" summary)
      (format "Task finished: %s" summary)))

  (gptel-make-tool
   :name "FinishTask"
   :function #'fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-finish-task
   :description "Call this tool when the task is complete.
SUMMARY: brief description of what was accomplished."
   :args '((:name "summary" :type string
            :description "Brief summary of what was accomplished"))
   :category "task"
   :async nil))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-abort-task
AbortTask abandons NOTHING: it only describes @ as "ABORTED: ..." and leaves the workspace in place, so a KILLed task can still be reviewed before `fanshi/ai-delegate--discard-task' throws it away
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-abort-task (summary)
    "Tool for the gptel-agent to signal task abortion.
SUMMARY is a brief description of why the task is aborted.
The working copy is deliberately NOT abandoned: partial work is frequently
still worth reviewing, so the commit is only marked with an ABORTED:
description and the workspace stays on disk.  Throwing it away is the
human's call, via `fanshi/ai-delegate--discard-task'."
    (when fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id
      (condition-case err
          (fanshi/call-jj nil "describe"
                          (format "--message=ABORTED: %s"
                                  (replace-regexp-in-string "[\r\n]+" " "
                                                            (string-trim summary))))
        (error
         (message "Warning: jj describe failed: %s" (error-message-string err))))
      (fanshi/ai-delegate--update-task-by-id fanshi/ai-delegate--current-task-id "KILL" summary)
      (format "Task aborted: %s\nYour workspace %s and its partial changes are kept for review; discard them with M-x fanshi/ai-delegate--discard-task."
              summary (abbreviate-file-name default-directory))))

  (gptel-make-tool
   :name "AbortTask"
   :function #'fanshi/ai-delegate--tool-abort-task
   :description "Call this tool when the task has to be abort.
SUMMARY: brief description of what was accomplished and tell us why"
   :args '((:name "reason" :type string
            :description "Brief explain of why the task was aborted"))
   :category "task"
   :async nil))
#+end_src
** Query Backlog
*** fanshi/ai-delegate--query-backlog
The read-side counterpart of `BreakdownTask': the backlog path is resolved
from `fanshi/gptel-backlog-path', never from a model-supplied argument, so the
Scrum Master reads its backlog without naming the file (and the sandbox needs
no exception for it).
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--query-backlog (&optional task-id)
    "Read the AI task backlog (`fanshi/gptel-backlog-path'); takes no file argument.
With no arguments, return one line per task: state, heading, org-id and key
properties.  With TASK-ID (an org-id), return that task's full entry —
heading, property drawer and body text — for when the summary line is not
enough to judge or decompose it."
    (if-let* ((path (fanshi/gptel-backlog-path))
              ((file-exists-p path)))
        ;; Same environment-error contract as `BreakdownTask': nil here is not
        ;; something the model can fix by rewording the call.
        (if task-id
            (save-match-data
              (if-let* ((marker (org-id-find (string-trim task-id))))
                  (with-current-buffer (marker-buffer marker)
                    (save-excursion
                      (goto-char marker)
                      (buffer-substring-no-properties
                       (line-beginning-position)
                       (org-end-of-subtree t t))))
                (error "Task %s not found in the backlog" task-id))
              (fanshi/ai-nightly--task-list path)))
      (error "AI task backlog file not found: %s" path)))

  (gptel-make-tool
   :name "QueryBacklog"
   :function #'fanshi/ai-delegate--query-backlog
   :description "Read the AI task backlog.  Takes no file argument: the destination is fixed.
With no arguments: one line per task — state, heading, org-id, and properties (priority, delegated-to, agent, depends-on, project).
With TASK-ID: that task's full entry, including its property drawer and body text; use this when the summary line is not enough to judge or decompose the task.
If the backlog is unavailable or TASK-ID is unknown, the call errors — escalate to the human; do not retry."
   :args '((:name "task-id" :type string
            :description "Optional org-id of a single task to fetch in full"
            :optional t))
   :category "agents"
   :async nil))
#+end_src
** Breakdown Task
*** fanshi/ai-delegate--breakdown-task
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-delegate--breakdown-task (parent-heading task-description subtasks &optional dependencies project)
    "Break down TASK-DESCRIPTION into SUBTASKS under PARENT-HEADING.

Tasks are written to the AI task backlog (`fanshi/gptel-backlog-path'), which
is deliberately NOT an argument: the destination is fixed, so the model cannot
point the tool at another file.
PARENT-HEADING: The heading under which to create subtasks (e.g., \"Implement feature X\").
TASK-DESCRIPTION: Description of the parent task (used to create the parent heading if it doesn't exist).
SUBTASKS: A list of plists, each with :title, :description, and optional :agent keys.
DEPENDENCIES: Optional plist mapping subtask titles to lists of subtask titles they depend on
(as decoded from the tool call's JSON object).
PROJECT: Optional directory the subtasks run in; stored as SCOPED_PROJECT
on each subtask so `Delegate` can find the right project root.

Creates the parent heading if it doesn't exist, then adds each subtask as a child
TODO entry with proper org-id and properties. Returns a summary of created tasks."
    (unless (fanshi/gptel-backlog-path)
      ;; The backlog path comes from org-gtd, so a nil here is an environment
      ;; problem, not something the model can fix by rewording the call.
      (error "AI task backlog unavailable (org-gtd not loaded); escalate to the human"))
    (unless subtasks
      (error "BREAKDOWN_TASK requires at least one subtask"))
    ;; gptel decodes the tool call's JSON with `json-read', which turns
    ;; JSON arrays into vectors; coerce to lists so the loops below can
    ;; iterate them (a vector here would otherwise crash or silently
    ;; iterate only once).
    (when (vectorp subtasks)
      (setq subtasks (seq-into subtasks 'list)))
    ;; A relative project path would otherwise resolve against the delegating
    ;; buffer's default-directory, so reject it up front.
    (when project
      (setq project (expand-file-name project))
      (unless (file-directory-p project)
        (error "Project directory %s does not exist" project)))
    ;; The tool call's JSON object arrives as a plist; convert to an alist.
    (unless (listp dependencies)
      (setq dependencies nil))
    (setq dependencies
          (delq nil (mapcar
                     (lambda (key)
                       (let ((val (plist-get dependencies key)))
                         (unless (listp val)
                           (error "Dependencies for task '%s' must be a list of subtask titles"
                                  (symbol-name key)))
                         (cons (symbol-name key) val)))
                     (doom-plist-keys dependencies))))
    ;; Flatten newlines in the parent heading before it is inserted, so a
    ;; multi-line model output cannot split a heading across lines.
    (setq parent-heading
          (string-trim (replace-regexp-in-string "[\n\r]+" " " parent-heading)))
    (let ((task-file (fanshi/gptel-backlog-path))
          titles)
      (dolist (subtask subtasks)
        (let ((title (plist-get subtask :title)))
          (unless (and title (string-trim title))
            (error "Subtask is missing a title"))
          ;; Flatten newlines the same way: a title with a newline would
          ;; corrupt the org structure when inserted.
          (setq title (string-trim (replace-regexp-in-string "[\n\r]+" " " title)))
          (setf (plist-get subtask :title) title)
          (when (member title titles)
            (error "Duplicate subtask title: %s" title))
          (push title titles)))
      (dolist (deps dependencies)
        (dolist (dep (cdr deps))
          (unless (member dep titles)
            (error "Dependency '%s' of task '%s' is not one of the subtasks created in this call"
                   dep (car deps))))
        (unless (member (car deps) titles)
          (error "Dependency owner '%s' is not one of the subtasks created in this call"
                 (car deps))))
      ;; Reject circular dependencies (e.g. A -> B -> A).
      (cl-labels ((visit (node visiting)
                    (when (member node visiting)
                      (error "Circular dependency detected involving task '%s'" node))
                    (dolist (dep (cdr (assoc node dependencies)))
                      (visit dep (cons node visiting)))))
        (dolist (title titles)
          (visit title nil)))
      (with-current-buffer (find-file-noselect task-file)
        (let ((created-tasks '())
              (task-title-map (make-hash-table :test 'equal))
              child-level
              ;; (title . marker) pairs for every heading in the file, used
              ;; for both the idempotency check and the parent lookup so both
              ;; use exact title comparison instead of a regex (which
              ;; mishandles tags, level boundaries and the keyword space).
              headings)
          ;; Capture the match data before `org-get-heading' clobbers it.
          (save-excursion
            (goto-char (point-min))
            (while (re-search-forward "^\\*+ " nil t)
              (let ((pos (match-beginning 0)))
                (when-let ((title (org-get-heading t t t)))
                  (push (cons title pos) headings)))))
          ;; Idempotency: a chatty model will happily break the same task down
          ;; twice.  Reject the whole call before writing anything if any
          ;; title already exists in the file, so the model sees the error
          ;; and does not retry.
          (dolist (title titles)
            (when (assoc title headings)
              (error "Subtask '%s' already exists in %s; breakdown is idempotent, do not retry"
                     title task-file)))
          ;; Find or create parent heading (exact title match, with or without
          ;; a TODO keyword)
          (unless (assoc parent-heading headings)
            ;; Create parent heading at end of file.  It is a plain (non-TODO)
            ;; container so the nightly dispatcher doesn't try to delegate it.
            (goto-char (point-max))
            (insert "\n\n* " parent-heading "\n"
                    (format "  %s\n" task-description))
            (org-back-to-heading))
          (setq child-level (1+ (save-excursion (org-back-to-heading) (org-outline-level))))
          (org-end-of-subtree t t)

          ;; Create each subtask
          (dolist (subtask subtasks)
            (let* ((title (plist-get subtask :title))
                   (desc (or (plist-get subtask :description) ""))
                   (agent (or (plist-get subtask :agent) "coding"))
                   task-id)
              ;; Create the subtask entry
              (insert (format "\n%s TODO %s\n" (make-string child-level ?*) title)
                      "  :PROPERTIES:\n"
                      "  :DELEGATED_TO: ai\n"
                      (format "  :AGENT: %s\n" agent)
                      (format "  :TIME_LIMIT: %s\n" fanshi/ai-task-time-limit)
                      (when project (format "  :SCOPED_PROJECT: %s\n" project))
                      "  :END:\n"
                      (mapconcat (lambda (l) (format "  %s" l))
                                 (split-string desc "\n") "\n"))
              (org-back-to-heading)
              ;; Store mapping
              (setq task-id (org-id-get nil t))
              (puthash title task-id task-title-map)
              (push (cons title task-id) created-tasks)
              ;; Point past this entry so the next insert appends after it.
              (org-end-of-subtree t t)))

          ;; Update dependencies with org-ids
          (dolist (deps dependencies)
            (let ((dep-ids (mapcar (lambda (d) (gethash d task-title-map)) (cdr deps))))
              (save-excursion
                (org-id-goto (gethash (car deps) task-title-map))
                (org-entry-put (point) "DEPENDS_ON" (string-join dep-ids ", ")))))
          (save-buffer)
          (format "Created %d subtasks under '%s':\n%s"
                  (length created-tasks)
                  parent-heading
                  (mapconcat (lambda (item)
                               (format "- %s (ID: %s)" (car item) (cdr item)))
                             created-tasks "\n"))))))
  (gptel-make-tool
   :name "BreakdownTask"
   :function #'fanshi/ai-delegate--breakdown-task
   :description "Break down a high-level task into subtasks and write them to the AI task backlog.
Finds the parent heading (matching its title, with or without a TODO keyword) or creates it as a plain container heading, then adds each subtask as a child TODO entry.
Each subtask gets a unique org-id, is marked for AI delegation (DELEGATED_TO: ai), and stores its agent preset (AGENT) and a default TIME_LIMIT.
DEPENDENCIES are stored as DEPENDS_ON properties (comma-separated org-ids).
The call is idempotent: if any subtask title already exists in the backlog, nothing is written and an error is returned — do not retry or reword to force a second entry.

The destination is always the AI task backlog; there is no file argument.
PARENT-HEADING: The title of the heading under which to create subtasks.
TASK-DESCRIPTION: Description of the parent task (used only if the heading must be created).
SUBTASKS: A list of subtask objects, each with:
  - title (string): The subtask title (must be unique within this call and not already present in the file)
  - description (string): Detailed description of what to do
  - agent (string, optional): A valid gptel agent preset name to delegate to (e.g. \"coding\", \"research\"); defaults to \"coding\"
DEPENDENCIES: Optional object mapping subtask titles to arrays of subtask titles they depend on.
Only subtasks created in this same call may be referenced, and the graph must be acyclic.
PROJECT: Optional absolute path of the project directory the subtasks run in (stored as SCOPED_PROJECT)."
   :args '((:name "parent-heading" :type string
            :description "The heading under which to create subtasks")
           (:name "task-description" :type string
            :description "Description of the parent/feature task")
           (:name "subtasks" :type array
            :description "List of subtask objects with title, description, and optional agent"
            :items '(:type object
                     :properties (:title (:type string)
                                  :description (:type string)
                                  :agent (:type string))))
           (:name "dependencies" :type object
            :description "Optional mapping of subtask titles to arrays of dependent task titles"
            :optional t)
           (:name "project" :type string
            :description "Optional absolute path of the project directory the subtasks run in"
            :optional t))
   :category "agents"
   :async nil))
#+end_src
*** fanshi/ai-breakdown
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/ai-breakdown (topic)
    "Have the Scrum Master decompose TOPIC into subtasks in the AI tasks file.
Opens the =scrum= preset in a dedicated gptel-agent buffer pointed at the GTD
directory and asks it to emit one =BreakdownTask= call.  The subtasks land in
the AI tasks org file as =TODO= entries, ready for `fanshi/ai-nightly--run'
or a manual =Delegate=.

With an active region TOPIC is the region text.  When point sits on an org
heading, its title becomes the parent heading and its body the description."
    (interactive
     (list (if (use-region-p)
               (buffer-substring-no-properties (region-beginning) (region-end))
             (read-string "Break down: "))))
    (when (string-blank-p topic)
      (user-error "Nothing to break down"))
    (let* ((tasks-file (org-gtd--path "org-gtd-ai-tasks"))
           (parent-heading (when (and (derived-mode-p 'org-mode) (org-at-heading-p))
                             (org-get-heading t t t)))
           ;; Only the subtree body becomes the description: the heading line is
           ;; already sent as PARENT-HEADING.
           (parent-desc (when parent-heading
                          (let ((body (save-excursion
                                        (org-back-to-heading)
                                        (buffer-substring-no-properties
                                         (line-end-position)
                                         (org-end-of-subtree t t)))))
                            (unless (string-blank-p body)
                              (string-trim body)))))
           (default-directory (file-name-directory tasks-file)))
      ;; `gptel-agent' forwards an active region as its own initial prompt;
      ;; deactivate the mark so TOPIC is not sent twice.
      (deactivate-mark)
      (let ((buf (gptel-agent default-directory 'scrum)))
        (with-current-buffer buf
          (goto-char (point-max))
          (insert (format
                   (concat "# Task Breakdown\n\n"
                           "Decompose the following into independently executable subtasks.\n\n"
                           "<task>\n%s\n</task>\n\n"
                           "Rules:\n"
                           "1. 3-7 subtasks. Each one is a single action a worker agent finishes in one sitting.\n"
                           "2. Call BreakdownTask exactly once with all subtasks and their dependencies, and do nothing else.\n"
                           "   (The only exception: if rule 5 applies, escalate in prose instead of calling BreakdownTask.)\n"
                           "3. Fill in each subtask's description: what to change, where, and the acceptance criteria.\n"
                           "4. Do NOT call Delegate. Decomposition only; dispatch happens later.\n"
                           "5. If the task is too vague to decompose safely, do not invent subtasks:\n"
                           "   escalate instead and name the specific decision you need.\n")
                   (string-trim topic)))
          (when parent-heading
            (goto-char (point-max))
            (insert (format
                     "Use parent-heading \"%s\"%s.\n"
                     parent-heading
                     (when parent-desc
                       (format " and task-description \"%s\""
                               (replace-regexp-in-string "\n" " " parent-desc))))))
          (gptel-send))
        (message "AI Breakdown: decomposing into %s" tasks-file)
        buf))))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-nightly--run
Spawn a Scrum Master agent to process all AI-delegated tasks.
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(after! (:and org-gtd gptel-agent)
  (defun fanshi/ai-nightly--task-list (file)
    "Return a plain-text summary of the tasks in the org FILE.
One line per task: state, heading, org-id and key properties."
    (with-temp-buffer
      (insert-file-contents file)
      (org-mode)
      (save-excursion
        (goto-char (point-min))
        (cl-loop while (re-search-forward
                        "^[ \t]*\\*+[ \t]+\\(TODO\\|DELEGATED\\|DONE\\|KILL\\)\\>[ \t]*\\(.*\\)" nil t)
                 for keyword = (match-string-no-properties 1)
                 for heading = (match-string-no-properties 2)
                 for id = (org-entry-get (point) "ID")
                 for delegated-to = (org-entry-get (point) "DELEGATED_TO")
                 for priority = (org-entry-get (point) "PRIORITY")
                 for agent = (org-entry-get (point) "AGENT")
                 for depends-on = (org-entry-get (point) "DEPENDS_ON")
                 for scoped-project = (org-entry-get (point) "SCOPED_PROJECT")
                 for gptel-model = (org-entry-get (point) "GPTEL_MODEL")
                 collect (format "- [%s] %s (ID: %s%s%s%s%s%s%s)"
                                 keyword heading id
                                 (when priority (format ", priority: %s" priority))
                                 (when delegated-to (format ", delegated-to: %s" delegated-to))
                                 (when agent (format ", agent: %s" agent))
                                 (when depends-on (format ", depends-on: %s" depends-on))
                                 (when scoped-project (format ", project: %s" scoped-project))
                                 (when gptel-model (format ", gptel-model: %s" gptel-model)))))))

  (defun fanshi/ai-nightly--run ()
    "Spawn a Scrum Master agent to process all AI-delegated tasks."
    (interactive)
    (let* ((ai-tasks-file (org-gtd--path "org-gtd-ai-tasks"))
           (ai-tasks-dir (file-name-directory ai-tasks-file)))
      (unless (file-exists-p ai-tasks-file)
        (error "AI Nightly: No AI tasks file at %s" ai-tasks-file))
      (message "AI Nightly: Starting Scrum Master orchestrator...")
      (with-current-buffer (get-buffer-create "*gptel-agent: scrum-nightly*")
        (gptel-agent ai-tasks-dir 'scrum)
        (goto-char (point-max))
        (insert (format
                 "\n\n# Nightly Task Dispatch\n\nCurrent tasks in %s:\n%s\n\nFor each TODO task, call Delegate with its task-id, an agent, and priority/time-limit/deadline/schedule when you know them.\nIf a TODO task describes a feature or goal rather than a single focused unit of work, break it down with BreakdownTask first, then delegate the resulting subtasks.\nWhen a task has an agent: property, use that agent.\nWhen a task has a project: property, pass it as the project of any BreakdownTask call for that task.\nSkip tasks already in DELEGATED, DONE, or KILL state.\nSkip tasks with depends-on: unless every listed task ID is DONE. Delegate also refuses to run a task whose depends-on are not DONE, so do not retry a blocked task.\nTasks with delegated-to: user are pending human input — skip them.\nTasks whose heading starts with \"[gptel]\" are queued gptel requests: replay each one by calling ReplayGptelTask with its task-id, in priority order (A first). The tool opens the right buffer, sends the request, marks the task DELEGATED, and returns only once the response finishes — so replay the next [gptel] task only after the previous call has returned; never run two [gptel] requests at the same time.\nAfter delegating all tasks, report a summary table.\n"
                 ai-tasks-file (fanshi/ai-nightly--task-list ai-tasks-file)))
        (gptel-send)
        (current-buffer)))))
#+end_src
** fanshi/ai-nightly--schedule
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(defvar fanshi/ai-nightly-start-time "23:00"
  "Time of day to auto-trigger the nightly processor.")

(defvar fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-start-time 900
  "Idle time to auto-trigger the nightly processor after `fanshi/ai-nightly-start-time'.")

(defvar fanshi/ai-nightly-timer nil
  "Timer object for the nightly processor.")

(defvar fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-timer nil
  "Timer object for the nightly processor.")

(defun fanshi/ai-nightly--schedule ()
  "Schedule the Scrum Master to run after idle for 15mins at the configured time."
  (when fanshi/ai-nightly-timer
    (cancel-timer fanshi/ai-nightly-timer))
  (when fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-timer
    (cancel-timer fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-timer))
  (setq fanshi/ai-nightly-timer
        (run-at-time fanshi/ai-nightly-start-time 86400
                     (lambda ()
                       (when fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-timer (cancel-timer fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-timer))
                       (setq fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-timer (run-with-idle-timer fanshi/ai-nightly-idle-start-time nil #'fanshi/ai-nightly--run)))))
  (message "AI Nightly: Scheduled to run at %s" fanshi/ai-nightly-start-time))

(fanshi/ai-nightly--schedule)
#+end_src

#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(defun fanshi/auth-source-pass--read-entry (entry)
  "Return a string with the file content of ENTRY."
  (with-file-contents! (expand-file-name (format "%s.gpg" entry) auth-source-pass-filename)
  (buffer-substring-no-properties (point-min) (point-max))))
(advice-add 'auth-source-pass--read-entry :override #'fanshi/auth-source-pass--read-entry)
#+end_src

** Sanitize stray bytes (e.g. em-dash as raw \342\200\224) that break gptel
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle no
(defun fanshi/gptel--recursive-sanitize (obj)
  "Recursively sanitize every string in OBJ."
  (cond
   ((stringp obj) (fanshi/gptel--sanitize-string obj))
   ((vectorp obj) (vconcat (mapcar #'fanshi/gptel--recursive-sanitize obj)))
   ((consp obj) (cons (fanshi/gptel--recursive-sanitize (car obj))
                      (fanshi/gptel--recursive-sanitize (cdr obj))))
   ((listp obj) (mapcar #'fanshi/gptel--recursive-sanitize obj))
   (t obj)))

;; Round-trip method used by sanitize-string:
(defun fanshi/gptel--sanitize-string (str)
  "Repair STR's encoding by utf-8 round-trip."
  (decode-coding-string (encode-coding-string str 'utf-8) 'utf-8))

(defun fanshi/gptel-curl--get-args-a (args)
  "Sanitize the request data in ARGS before it goes to curl."
  (let* ((data (car args))
         (cleaned-data (fanshi/gptel--recursive-sanitize data)))
    (cons cleaned-data (cdr args))))

(advice-add 'gptel-curl--get-args :filter-args #'fanshi/gptel-curl--get-args-a)
#+end_src

** make gptel--stream-convert-markdown->org gptel-post-response-function hook buffer local
- https://github.com/karthink/gptel/pull/1488
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes :lexical t
(after! gptel
  (defun fanshi/gptel--stream-convert-markdown->org-a (start-marker)
    "Markdown-to-Org converter for a stream of chunks.

Called with successive chunks of the text stream, it parses Markdown to Org
continuously. START-MARKER identifies the corresponding process when cleaning up
after."
    (letrec ((in-src-block nil)           ;explicit nil for BUG #183
             (in-org-src-block nil)
             (temp-buf ; NOTE: `generate-new-buffer' once Emacs 27.1 is dropped
              (gptel--temp-buffer " *gptel-temp*"))
             (start-pt (make-marker))
             (ticks-total 0)      ;MAYBE let-bind case-fold-search here?
             (cleanup-fn
              (lambda (beg _)
                (when (and (equal beg (marker-position start-marker))
                           (eq (current-buffer) (marker-buffer start-marker)))
                  (when (buffer-live-p (get-buffer temp-buf))
                    (set-marker start-pt nil)
                    (kill-buffer temp-buf))
                  (remove-hook 'gptel-post-response-functions cleanup-fn t)))))
      (add-hook 'gptel-post-response-functions cleanup-fn nil t)
      (lambda (str)
        (let ((noop-p) (ticks 0))
          (with-current-buffer (get-buffer temp-buf)
            (save-excursion (goto-char (point-max)) (insert str))
            (when (marker-position start-pt) (goto-char start-pt))
            (when in-src-block (setq ticks ticks-total))
            (save-excursion
              (while (re-search-forward "`\\|\\*\\{1,2\\}\\|_\\|^#+" nil t)
                (pcase (match-string 0)
                  ("`"
                   ;; Count consecutive backticks
                   (backward-char)
                   (while (and (char-after) (eq (char-after) ?`))
                     (forward-char)
                     (if in-src-block (cl-decf ticks) (cl-incf ticks)))
                   ;; Set the parser's verbatim state
                   (if (and (eobp)
                            ;; Heuristic: a response ending in ^``` needs no more
                            ;; input. FIXME: false positives possible.
                            (not (save-excursion (beginning-of-line)
                                                 (looking-at "^```$"))))
                       ;; End of input: more backticks may follow, so wait
                       (progn (setq noop-p t) (set-marker start-pt (match-beginning 0)))
                     ;; Reached a non-backtick character
                     (cond
                      ;; Ticks balanced: end the src block
                      ((= ticks 0)
                       (progn (setq in-src-block nil)
                              (gptel--replace-source-marker ticks-total 'end)))
                      ;; Positive ticks: start a src block
                      ((and (> ticks 0) (not in-src-block))
                       (setq ticks-total ticks
                             in-src-block t)
                       (gptel--replace-source-marker ticks-total))
                      ;; Negative ticks, or already in a block: reset
                      (t (setq ticks ticks-total)))))
                  ;; Headings and misguided #+begin_src text
                  ((and (guard (and (eq (char-before) ?#) (or (not in-src-block) in-org-src-block)))
                        heading)
                   (if in-org-src-block
                       ;; Inside an Org src block: look for #+end_src
                       (cond
                        ((< (- (point-max) (point)) 8) ;too little to close the block
                         (setq noop-p t) (set-marker start-pt (match-beginning 0)))
                        ((looking-at "\\+end_src") ;Close the Org src block
                         (setq in-src-block nil in-org-src-block nil)))
                     ;; Otherwise check for Markdown headings, or #+begin_src
                     (cond
                      ((eobp)       ;not enough information about the heading yet
                       (setq noop-p t) (set-marker start-pt (match-beginning 0)))
                      ((looking-at "[[:space:]]") ;Markdown heading -> Org heading
                       (delete-region (line-beginning-position) (point))
                       (insert (make-string (length heading) ?*)))
                      ((< (- (point-max) (point)) 11) ;too little to test for a block
                       (setq noop-p t) (set-marker start-pt (match-beginning 0)))
                      ((looking-at "\\+begin_src ") ;LLM switched to Org src blocks
                       (setq in-src-block t in-org-src-block t)))))
                  ;; Emphasis, bold and bullet items
                  ((and "**" (guard (not in-src-block)))
                   (cond
                    ;; TODO unclear why this branch was needed
                    ;; ((looking-at "\\*\\(?:[[:word:]]\\|\s\\)") (delete-char 1))

                    ;; Looking back at "w**" or " **"
                    ((looking-back "\\(?:[[:word:][:punct:]\n]\\|\s\\)\\*\\{2\\}"
                                   (max (- (point) 3) (point-min)))
                     (delete-char -1))))
                  ((and "*" (guard (not in-src-block)))
                   (if (eobp)
                       ;; Not enough information about the "*" yet
                       (progn (setq noop-p t) (set-marker start-pt (match-beginning 0)))
                     ;; "*" is emphasis or a bullet
                     (save-match-data
                       (save-excursion
                         (ignore-errors (backward-char 2))
                         (cond
                          ((and     ; At bob, underscore/asterisk followed by word
                            (or (and (bobp) (looking-at "\\(?:_\\|\\*\\)\\([^[:space:][:punct:]]\\|$\\)"))
                                (looking-at ; word followed by underscore/asterisk
                                 "[^[:space:]\n]\\(?:_\\|\\*\\)\\(?:[[:space:][:punct:]]\\|$\\)")
                                (looking-at ; underscore/asterisk followed by word
                                 "\\(?:[[:space:]]\\)\\(?:_\\|\\*\\)\\([^[:space:]]\\|$\\)"))
                            (not (looking-at "[[:punct:]]\\(?:_\\|\\*\\)[[:punct:]]")))
                           ;; Emphasis -> slashes
                           (forward-char (if (bobp) 1 2)) (delete-char -1) (insert "/"))
                          ((or (and (bobp) (looking-at "\\*[[:space:]]"))
                               (looking-at "\\(?:$\\|\\`\\)\n\\*[[:space:]]"))
                           ;; Bullet -> hyphen
                           (forward-char (if (bobp) 1 2)) (delete-char -1) (insert "-"))))))))))
            (if noop-p
                (buffer-substring (point) start-pt)
              (prog1 (buffer-substring (point) (point-max))
                (set-marker start-pt (point-max)))))))))
  (advice-add 'gptel--stream-convert-markdown->org :override #'fanshi/gptel--stream-convert-markdown->org-a))
#+end_src

* Browse Url
** fanshi/clock-in-when-at-link-in-gtd-actions
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! org
  (defun fanshi/clock-in-when-at-link-in-gtd-actions ()
    (save-excursion
      (when (derived-mode-p 'org-agenda-mode) (org-agenda-switch-to))
      (when (and (derived-mode-p 'org-mode)
                 (eq buffer-file-name (org-gtd--path org-gtd-default-file-name))
                 (eq 'link (org-element-type (org-element-context))))
        (org-back-to-heading)
        (let ((props (org-entry-properties)))
          (when (and (string= (alist-get "ORG_GTD" props nil nil #'equal) "Actions")
                     (string= (alist-get "TODO" props nil nil #'equal) "NEXT"))
            (org-clock-in)))))))
#+end_src
** Browse Url handlers
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(after! org
  (defun fanshi/mpv-youtube (url &optional _new-window)
    "Browse URL using mpv for YouTube."
    (fanshi/clock-in-when-at-link-in-gtd-actions)
    (call-process "mpv" nil 0 nil url
                  "--ytdl-format=bestvideo[height<=?1080]+bestaudio/best"
                  "--ontop"))
  (defun fanshi/eww-browse-url-with-clock-in (url &optional new-window)
    (fanshi/clock-in-when-at-link-in-gtd-actions)
    (eww-browse-url url new-window))
  (setq browse-url-handlers '(("youtube\\.com\\|youtu\\.be" . fanshi/mpv-youtube)))
  (setq browse-url-browser-function #'fanshi/eww-browse-url-with-clock-in))

**  jj color for shell-command
adapted from
- Source :: https://stackoverflow.com/a/72132446
- Posted by :: Chris
- Retrieved :: 2026-06-25  License - CC BY-SA 4.0
#+begin_src emacs-lisp :tangle yes
(defun fanshi/ansi-colorize-jj-shell-command-output-a (cmd &rest _args)
  "Colorize `shell-command' output."
  (when (string-prefix-p "jj" (s-trim cmd))
    (cl-loop for buf in (buffer-list)
             when (or (string-prefix-p " *Echo Area" (buffer-name buf))
                      (string-prefix-p "*Shell Command" (buffer-name buf)))
             do (with-current-buffer buf
                  (ansi-color-apply-on-region (point-min) (point-max))))))

(advice-add 'shell-command :after #'fanshi/ansi-colorize-jj-shell-command-output-a)
#+end_src

(after! vc-jj

        (append '(JJ) (remq 'JJ vc-handled-backends))))
